Appendix A Notation and derivations

A.1 Colourings, weights and the exact across-key law. The detector scores tuples u=(c,v)u = (c, v), a context cc of hh tokens and a token vv, over a vocabulary of VV tokens. The key KK colours each seed class (a context, or the class of contexts the seeding hash merges) by a uniformly random mm-subset of green tokens, m=⌊γV⌋m = \lfloor \gamma V \rfloor, g=m/Vg = m/V, independently across classes: the fixed-size split of KGW’s seeded permutation. Under the independent-colour model each tuple is green independently with probability gg; it is the implementation surrogate of Studies 1–4 (the colour is the indicator U(key,u)<gU(\text{key}, u) < g of a seeded hash) and the model Study 1 compares with the fixed-size split on its core subset. With GK(u)∈{0,1}G_K(u) \in \{0, 1\} the colour and ww fixed coefficients over tuples (a probability weight, or the colour coefficients below), δK(w)=∑uw(u)(GK(u)−g)\delta_K(w) = \sum_u w(u)(G_K(u) - g) has 𝔼KδK=0\mathbb{E}_K \delta_K = 0. Under the fixed-size split VarGK(u)=g(1−g)\operatorname{Var} G_K(u) = g(1-g), two distinct tokens of one class have covariance −g(1−g)/(V−1)-g(1-g)/(V-1) and tokens of different classes are independent, so with A=∑uw(u)2A = \sum_u w(u)^2, Ac=∑vw(c,v)2A_c = \sum_v w(c, v)^2 and B=∑cw(c)2B = \sum_c w(c)^2, VarKδK(w)=g(1−g)[A−1V−1∑c(w(c)2−Ac)]=g(1−g)V−1(VA−B);\operatorname{Var}_K \delta_K(w) = g(1-g)\left[A - \frac{1}{V-1}\sum_c \bigl(w(c)^2 - A_c\bigr)\right] = \frac{g(1-g)}{V-1}\,(V A - B); under independent colours VarKδK(w)=g(1−g)A\operatorname{Var}_K \delta_K(w) = g(1-g) A. The ratio is (V−1/Ccond)/(V−1)(V - 1/C_{\mathrm{cond}})/(V-1) with Ccond=A/BC_{\mathrm{cond}} = A/B, and the relative reduction (1/Ccond−1)/(V−1)(1/C_{\mathrm{cond}} - 1)/(V-1) is 00 when every class puts its mass on one tuple and 11 when every class is spread uniformly over the vocabulary. A detector centred at γ\gamma rather than gg carries the offset into its level: in zz-units the exact across-key mean of the shift is μδ=(g−γ)∑uŵz(u)\mu_\delta = (g - \gamma)\sum_u \hat w_z(u), zero when γV\gamma V is an integer. The single-context case (B=1B = 1) is Unigram’s Lemma C.21, γ(1−γ)/(V−1)⋅(V∥pt∥2−1)\gamma(1-\gamma)/(V-1)\cdot(V\lVert p_t\rVert^2 - 1), Lemma F.2 being the general finite-population variance (X. Zhao et al. 2024, 31, 36); the same pair for the mean zz under the raw and distinct rules, with a fixed-size and an independent colouring, is stated and validated by the concurrent course project (Yukhimchuk 2026); the hypergeometric green count among the alive codes of a finite vocabulary is Jovanović et al.’s Equation 9 (Jovanović et al. 2025). The identities are checked at the realised weights in every cell, R3 (1098 checks, 0 gross failures) and S3 (3,468, 0), with the known-mean variance estimator s02=K−1∑k(q̂k−g)2s_0^2 = K^{-1}\sum_k (\hat q_k - g)^2 of the per-key mean green fraction against the identity g(1−g)Ag(1-g)A (independent colours; its SE from the exact fourth moment of a weighted sum of independent Bernoulli variables, κ4=g(1−g)(1−6g(1−g))\kappa_4 = g(1-g)(1 - 6g(1-g))) or g(1−g)(VA−B)/(V−1)g(1-g)(VA - B)/(V-1) (fixed-size split; a key-bootstrap SE), and a gross failure declared at |s02−σexact2|>6SE+τ|s_0^2 - \sigma^2_{\mathrm{exact}}| > 6\,\mathrm{SE} + \tau with the scale-aware tolerance τ=10−9g(1−g)A\tau = 10^{-9} g(1-g) A; for Gumbel-max the same check takes the per-key mean score s‾k\bar s_k against 𝔼s‾=1\mathbb{E}\,\bar s = 1 with the identity VarKs‾k=A\operatorname{Var}_K \bar s_k = A (the scores −ln(1−U)-\ln(1-U) are Exp(1), unit variance), the SE from the exponential fourth moment (κ4=6\kappa_4 = 6; the squared terms are unbounded) and τ=10−9A\tau = 10^{-9} A (Appendix B).

The weights. Under the raw rule, with niun_{iu} the number of scored positions of text ii at tuple uu and Mi=∑uniu>0M_i = \sum_u n_{iu} > 0, three normalisations of the count table are used and named apart: the colour coefficients ŵz(u)=Neff−1∑iniu/Miγ(1−γ)\hat w_z(u) = N_{\mathrm{eff}}^{-1}\sum_i n_{iu}/\sqrt{M_i\gamma(1-\gamma)}, under which the mean zz is ∑uŵz(u)(Gk(u)−γ)\sum_u \hat w_z(u)(G_k(u) - \gamma) and VarK(z‾)=g(1−g)∑uŵz(u)2\operatorname{Var}_K(\bar z) = g(1-g)\sum_u \hat w_z(u)^2 under independent colours; the fraction weights ŵq(u)=Neff−1∑iniu/Mi\hat w_q(u) = N_{\mathrm{eff}}^{-1}\sum_i n_{iu}/M_i, a probability weight with q̂k=∑uŵq(u)Gk(u)\hat q_k = \sum_u \hat w_q(u) G_k(u) exactly; and the standardised weights bi(u)=niu/Mib_i(u) = n_{iu}/\sqrt{M_i} per text, b(u)=Neff−1∑ibi(u)b(u) = N_{\mathrm{eff}}^{-1}\sum_i b_i(u) per set, which multiply a colour standardised to unit variance, (G−g)/g(1−g)(G - g)/\sqrt{g(1-g)}, or a Gumbel-max score minus its mean, so that ŵz=b/γ(1−γ)\hat w_z = b/\sqrt{\gamma(1-\gamma)} and, at g=γg = \gamma under independent colours (or for unit-variance scores), CovK(zi,zj)=∑ubi(u)bj(u)\operatorname{Cov}_K(z_i, z_j) = \sum_u b_i(u)\,b_j(u) and VarK(zi)=∑uniu2/Mi\operatorname{Var}_K(z_i) = \sum_u n_{iu}^2/M_i; A.5’s cross-set covariance is written in bb. A repeated-context rule replaces the counts by the retained set UL(X)U_L(X) of a text of length LL and gives the occupancy weights au(L)=𝔼[1{u∈UL(X)}/ML]a_u(L) = \mathbb{E}[\mathbf 1\{u \in U_L(X)\}/M_L] on {ML>0}\{M_L > 0\}, a text with ML=0M_L = 0 contributing no detection. Their collision ∑uau(L)2\sum_u a_u(L)^2 is not monotone in LL: a process that draws one of three equally likely prefixes (ai,bi,z)(a_i, b_i, z) with six distinct private tokens ai,bia_i, b_i has, under the distinct rule at h=0h = 0, collision 6⋅(1/6)2=1/66\cdot(1/6)^2 = 1/6 at L=2L = 2 and 6⋅(1/9)2+(1/3)2=5/276\cdot(1/9)^2 + (1/3)^2 = 5/27 at L=3L = 3. The retained set reaches the vocabulary only under a coverage condition: under the distinct rule a document process every path of which eventually covers the same fixed support of ss tokens has limiting occupancy weights uniform on that support, A=1/sA = 1/s and B=1B = 1 at h=0h = 0, hence VarKδK→g(1−g)(V−s)/(s(V−1))\operatorname{Var}_K \delta_K \to g(1-g)(V-s)/(s(V-1)), zero only at s=Vs = V (Unigram’s Remark E.2, whose mm counts the unique tokens, our ss, divided by s2s^2 (X. Zhao et al. 2024, 34)); under the first rule the limit is set by the limiting occupancy weights themselves (at h=0h = 0 that rule keeps one token per text, so an i.i.d. two-token law (0.9,0.1)(0.9, 0.1) with V=2V = 2 and m=1m = 1 has limiting variance 4/254/25 although every text covers the vocabulary). Three collision quantities stay apart: Ccond=A/BC_{\mathrm{cond}} = A/B of the scored weights, which sets the fixed-size ratio; the tuple collision Ch=∑uph(u)2C_h = \sum_u p_h(u)^2 of the law (for an i.i.d. law Ch=C0h+1C_h = C_0^{h+1}); and the between-text collision Ã(h)=NN−1[A(ŵq)−N−1meaniAiq]\tilde A(h) = \frac{N}{N-1}\bigl[A(\hat w_q) - N^{-1}\operatorname{mean}_i A_i^q\bigr] with Aiq=∑u(niu/Mi)2A_i^q = \sum_u (n_{iu}/M_i)^2, the average over pairs of texts of ∑uwi(u)wj(u)\sum_u w_i(u) w_j(u).

A.2 The first-order predictor. For a cell with NeffN_{\mathrm{eff}} texts and KK keys the model is zi=δk+eiz_i = \delta_k + e_i with δk∼𝒩(μδ,ρ‾)\delta_k \sim \mathcal N(\mu_\delta, \bar\rho) common to a key’s texts and residuals independent across texts with one variance σ2\sigma^2, so αk=Φ‾((zα−δk)/σ)\alpha_k = \bar\Phi((z_\alpha - \delta_k)/\sigma) and VarK(α̂k)=Var(αk)+𝔼[αk(1−αk)]/Neff\operatorname{Var}_K(\hat\alpha_k) = \operatorname{Var}(\alpha_k) + \mathbb{E}[\alpha_k(1-\alpha_k)]/N_{\mathrm{eff}} for one common threshold; with per-text thresholds τi\tau_i (Gumbel-max) the finite-text term is 𝔼D[meaniπi(D)(1−πi(D))]/Neff\mathbb{E}_D[\operatorname{mean}_i \pi_i(D)(1-\pi_i(D))]/N_{\mathrm{eff}} with πi(D)=P(Zi>τi∣D)\pi_i(D) = P(Z_i > \tau_i \mid D) the rejection probability of text ii given the common shift D=δkD = \delta_k and the outer expectation over the shift, computed as (𝔼[αk]−meaniP(Zi>τi,Zi′>τi))/Neff(\mathbb{E}[\alpha_k] - \operatorname{mean}_i P(Z_i > \tau_i, Z_i' > \tau_i))/N_{\mathrm{eff}} for two texts sharing the shift and the same threshold; it vanishes at σ=0\sigma = 0, where πi(D)\pi_i(D) is an indicator, whereas the marginal probabilities P(Zi>τi)P(Z_i > \tau_i) would not give it, and 𝔼[αk(1−αk)]/Neff\mathbb{E}[\alpha_k(1-\alpha_k)]/N_{\mathrm{eff}} with the averaged level would not vanish either. Its inputs are exact second moments at the realised weights under the cell’s colouring: μδ\mu_\delta as above; ρ‾=NeffNeff−1[VarK(z‾)−Neff−1meaniVarK(zi)]\bar\rho = \frac{N_{\mathrm{eff}}}{N_{\mathrm{eff}}-1}\bigl[\operatorname{Var}_K(\bar z) - N_{\mathrm{eff}}^{-1}\operatorname{mean}_i \operatorname{Var}_K(z_i)\bigr], the average over pairs i≠ji \ne j of CovK(zi,zj)\operatorname{Cov}_K(z_i, z_j), which removes the finite-text part the realised-weight identity carries; σ2=meaniVarK(zi)−ρ‾+NeffNeff−1meani(μi−μδ)2\sigma^2 = \operatorname{mean}_i \operatorname{Var}_K(z_i) - \bar\rho + \frac{N_{\mathrm{eff}}}{N_{\mathrm{eff}}-1}\operatorname{mean}_i(\mu_i - \mu_\delta)^2 with μi=(g−γ)Mi/(γ(1−γ))\mu_i = (g-\gamma)\sqrt{M_i/(\gamma(1-\gamma))}. In closed form 𝔼[αk]=Φ‾(t)\mathbb{E}[\alpha_k] = \bar\Phi(t) with t=(zα−μδ)/ρ‾+σ2t = (z_\alpha - \mu_\delta)/\sqrt{\bar\rho + \sigma^2}, and 𝔼[αk2]\mathbb{E}[\alpha_k^2] is the bivariate normal orthant probability of two scores sharing the shift, at correlation ρ‾/(ρ‾+σ2)\bar\rho/(\bar\rho + \sigma^2), through Owen’s TT function (Owen 1956); σ=0\sigma = 0 gives correlation 11 and αk=1{δk>zα}\alpha_k = \mathbf 1\{\delta_k > z_\alpha\}. The identities determine means and covariances only: the common shift, the common residual variance, the conditional independence and the normality are assumptions, and averaging covariances before the nonlinear tail is a further approximation. The theory share is Var(αk)\operatorname{Var}(\alpha_k) over the total; a cell enters an adequacy rule at a share of at least 0.75 and, in Studies 1 and 2, a mean MM of at least 30. Diagnostics: the unit-variance variant (σ=1\sigma = 1); the delta-method limit φ((zα−μδ)/σ)ρ‾/σ\varphi((z_\alpha - \mu_\delta)/\sigma)\sqrt{\bar\rho}/\sigma; the observed within-key variance against σ2\sigma^2; a flag when σ2\sigma^2 is materially negative or non-finite. The pairwise Gaussian predictor drops the common-factor and homogeneity assumptions and keeps normality: on two disjoint seeded subsamples of 300 texts it uses the exact across-key covariance matrix of the scores (the bilinear form of the cell’s colouring), VarK(α̂k)=meaniπi(1−πi)/Neff+(1−1/Neff)meani≠j[P(zi>zα,zj>zα)−πiπj]\operatorname{Var}_K(\hat\alpha_k) = \operatorname{mean}_i \pi_i(1-\pi_i)/N_{\mathrm{eff}} + (1 - 1/N_{\mathrm{eff}})\operatorname{mean}_{i\ne j}[P(z_i > z_\alpha, z_j > z_\alpha) - \pi_i\pi_j] with πi=Φ‾((zα−μi)/Vi)\pi_i = \bar\Phi((z_\alpha - \mu_i)/\sqrt{V_i}), and records the covariance heterogeneity of the subsample; the simulated-shift predictor of Study 2 draws the shift from the realised weights with fresh ideal colourings (the heaviest tuples exactly, the remainder Gaussian, the draws rescaled to variance ρ‾\bar\rho) and averages the common-factor model over that discrete sample, a moment-matched shape diagnostic. For Gumbel-max the same model carries each text’s exact Gamma threshold τi\tau_i in zz-units: 𝔼[αk]=meaniΦ‾(ai)\mathbb{E}[\alpha_k] = \operatorname{mean}_i \bar\Phi(a_i) with ai=(τi−μδ)/ρ‾+σ2a_i = (\tau_i - \mu_\delta)/\sqrt{\bar\rho + \sigma^2} and the second moment the mean over pairs of the orthant probability at the distinct thresholds, exact within the Gaussian predictor for every σ2≥0\sigma^2 \ge 0 (the score law itself is Exp(1); the Gaussian residual is a measured approximation), with Vars=1\operatorname{Var} s = 1 and μδ=0\mu_\delta = 0.

A.3 The hypotheses as registered. H-length: under the raw rule ρ‾\bar\rho grows with MM while the occupancy weights stay concentrated, and under the repeated-context rules it drifts toward the plateau the covered support sets; R4 tests ρ‾\bar\rho non-decreasing in LL within 2(seL+seL′)2(\mathrm{se}_L + \mathrm{se}_{L'}) for adjacent lengths of every core chain on the synthetic laws (text-bootstrap SEs as tolerances; the other rules reported without a rule), R4b the support control’s ρ‾\bar\rho within three text-bootstrap SEs of its analytic limit, and S4 the observed SDK(α̂k)\mathrm{SD}_K(\hat\alpha_k) non-decreasing in LL within 2(seL+seL′)2(\mathrm{se}_L + \mathrm{se}_{L'}) of the key-bootstrap SEs in every real-text chain under the raw rule at γ=0.25\gamma = 0.25 and α=0.01\alpha = 0.01. H-width: the tuple collision C(ph)=∑uph(u)2C(p_h) = \sum_u p_h(u)^2 of a projectively consistent raw tuple law is non-increasing in hh (a finer partition), which says nothing by itself about the empirical between-text collision or the observed SD; R5 tests Ã\tilde A non-increasing in hh within 2(seh+seh+1)2(\mathrm{se}_h + \mathrm{se}_{h+1}) in every core chain, and S5 the observed SD non-increasing in hh at the longest length under the raw and distinct rules. The occupancy weights of different widths are not a projective family and M=L−hM = L - h changes with hh, so the width rules are expected, not implied.

A.4 Calibration and the comparators as thresholds on pp. Each method is a threshold tt on the published test’s per-text pp-value (Φ‾(z)\bar\Phi(z) of the zz-test centred at γ\gamma; the exact Gamma upper tail for Gumbel-max), with strict rejection p<tp < t. Nominal: t=αt = \alpha. AutoMark’s multiplier: t=αβt = \alpha\beta with β\beta = 0.05, Proposition 1’s correction of a detector sound over the key (Gloaguen, Staab, and Vechev 2026); the prerequisite holds exactly for the distinct-rule Gumbel-max test under ideal randomness, asymptotically for the distinct-rule zz-test, and fails under the raw rule, where a repeated tuple makes a text’s pp-value a function of one colouring. Empirical multiplier (scheme level, on the calibration set CC of NCN_C texts with KfitK_{\mathrm{fit}} = 1,000 fitting keys disjoint from the evaluation keys): tk*t^*_k is the (⌊αNC⌋+1)(\lfloor\alpha N_C\rfloor + 1)-th smallest pp of fitting key kk, the largest threshold whose realised level on CC is at most α\alpha; tempt_{\mathrm{emp}} is the smaller of α\alpha and the (⌊βKfit⌋+1)(\lfloor\beta K_{\mathrm{fit}}\rfloor + 1)-th smallest tk*t^*_k, so that at most ⌊βKfit⌋\lfloor\beta K_{\mathrm{fit}}\rfloor fitting keys exceed α\alpha on CC, and κ̂=α/temp\hat\kappa = \alpha/t_{\mathrm{emp}}; it runs neither AutoMark’s Algorithm 2 nor its Algorithm 3. Pooled: tpoolt_{\mathrm{pool}} is the (⌊αNCKfit⌋+1)(\lfloor\alpha N_C K_{\mathrm{fit}}\rfloor + 1)-th smallest of the fitting keys’ pp-values on CC, the largest threshold whose key-averaged level on CC is at most α\alpha, not capped at α\alpha. Per-key conformal: tkt_k is the jj-th smallest of key kk’s pp-values on the first NcalN_{\mathrm{cal}} calibration texts, j=⌊α(Ncal+1)⌋j = \lfloor\alpha(N_{\mathrm{cal}} + 1)\rfloor in exact rational arithmetic (j=0j = 0 never rejects); exchangeability of the calibration texts and a fresh text gives the marginal bound j/(Ncal+1)j/(N_{\mathrm{cal}} + 1) on average over the calibration draw, and i.i.d. continuous scores give the level Beta(j,Ncal+1−j)\mathrm{Beta}(j, N_{\mathrm{cal}} + 1 - j) given the calibration set (Vovk 2012), the reference law whose adequacy T1 measures. Per-key tolerance (Vovk 2012, Proposition 2b): the jδj_\delta-th smallest with jδ=max{j≥1:P(Bin(Ncal,α)≤j−1)≤δ}j_\delta = \max\{j \ge 1 : P(\mathrm{Bin}(N_{\mathrm{cal}}, \alpha) \le j - 1) \le \delta\} and δ\delta = 0.05, so that a fixed key’s population level exceeds α\alpha with probability at most δ\delta over calibration draws; where no such jj exists the method abstains. For discrete scores both per-key methods are conservative (a tie at the order statistic is not rejected). The null-side cost of a threshold is z(t)−zαz(t) - z_\alpha with z(t)=Φ‾−1(t)z(t) = \bar\Phi^{-1}(t).

A.5 The calibrated-threshold and transfer predictor. On a text set SS key kk gives zi=DkS+eiz_i = D_k^S + e_i, the residuals i.i.d. 𝒩(0,σS2)\mathcal N(0, \sigma_S^2) across texts and keys. Inputs, exact at the realised weights: ρ‾S\bar\rho_S as in A.2; σS2=meaniVi−ρ‾S\sigma_S^2 = \operatorname{mean}_i V_i - \bar\rho_S with Vi=∑uniu2/MiV_i = \sum_u n_{iu}^2/M_i; ρCE=∑ubC(u)bE(u)\rho_{CE} = \sum_u b_C(u)\, b_E(u), the mean over cross pairs of CovK(zi,zj)\operatorname{Cov}_K(z_i, z_j), with bb the set’s document-averaged standardised weight N−1∑iniu/MiN^{-1}\sum_i n_{iu}/\sqrt{M_i} (A.1) over the integer tuple ids, exact under independent colours at g=γg = \gamma or for unit-variance scores, because the hash is a function of the integer ids alone. A per-key threshold with order index jj sits at DkC+aD_k^C + a with a=σCΦ−1(U)a = \sigma_C\Phi^{-1}(U) and U∼Beta(Ncal+1−j,j)U \sim \mathrm{Beta}(N_{\mathrm{cal}} + 1 - j, j), and the realised level on EE’s law is ℓ=Φ‾((X+σCΦ−1(U))/σE)\ell = \bar\Phi\bigl((X + \sigma_C\Phi^{-1}(U))/\sigma_E\bigr) with X=DC−DE∼𝒩(0,v)X = D^C - D^E \sim \mathcal N(0, v) and v=ρ‾C+ρ‾E−2ρCEv = \bar\rho_C + \bar\rho_E - 2\rho_{CE}. The estimate of vv obeys an exact identity: since ρ‾S=AS−σS2/nS\bar\rho_S = A_S - \sigma_S^2/n_S for each set with AS=∥bS∥2A_S = \lVert b_S\rVert^2, v+τv=∥bC−bE∥2≥0v + \tau_v = \lVert b_C - b_E\rVert^2 \ge 0 with τv=σC2/Ncal+σE2/NE\tau_v = \sigma_C^2/N_{\mathrm{cal}} + \sigma_E^2/N_E, so a negative estimate within that floor is noise around a non-negative quantity (set to 00, recorded, unflagged) and a value below −τv-\tau_v is impossible and flagged, the pair ineligible (the implemented comparison is vraw<−τv(1+10−9)−10−300v_{\mathrm{raw}} < -\tau_v(1 + 10^{-9}) - 10^{-300}, a relative slack and an absolute guard with no practical effect, as the Study 3 protocol’s integrity section states). Given UU the integral over XX is closed (the mean Φ‾(a/s)\bar\Phi(a/s) with s=σE2+vs = \sqrt{\sigma_E^2 + v}; the second moment through Owen’s TT at correlation v/(σE2+v)v/(\sigma_E^2 + v)); the UU integral is a 20,000-node midpoint rule on the probability scale; the finite-evaluation term 𝔼[ℓ(1−ℓ)]/NE\mathbb{E}[\ell(1-\ell)]/N_E is added for the conditional SD, and the theory share is the population variance over the total. Two predicted shares of keys above a level aa are different estimands: the population share P(ℓ>a)P(\ell > a), in closed form, and the finite-evaluation share P(count>⌊aNE⌋)=𝔼[BinomSF(⌊aNE⌋;NE,ℓ)]P(\mathrm{count} > \lfloor a N_E\rfloor) = \mathbb{E}[\mathrm{BinomSF}(\lfloor a N_E\rfloor; N_E, \ell)], which a measured share over NEN_E texts estimates; through BinomSF(m;NE,ℓ)=P(W<ℓ)\mathrm{BinomSF}(m; N_E, \ell) = P(W < \ell) with W∼Beta(m+1,NE−m)W \sim \mathrm{Beta}(m + 1, N_E - m) the latter is 𝔼U,W[Φ((σEΦ‾−1(W)−σCΦ−1(U))/v)]\mathbb{E}_{U, W}\bigl[\Phi\bigl((\sigma_E\bar\Phi^{-1}(W) - \sigma_C\Phi^{-1}(U))/\sqrt v\bigr)\bigr], a smooth integrand on two probability scales integrated by a midpoint rule in each (1,000 nodes per scale for the per-key methods, 4,000 nodes of WW for a fixed threshold, where the integrand is Φ‾((tz−σEΦ‾−1(W))/ρ‾E)\bar\Phi((t_z - \sigma_E\bar\Phi^{-1}(W))/\sqrt{\bar\rho_E}); at v=0v = 0 the share is 𝔼U[BinomSF(m;NE,ℓ(U))]\mathbb{E}_U[\mathrm{BinomSF}(m; N_E, \ell(U))] on the nodes of UU, and at ρ‾E=0\bar\rho_E = 0 the binomial tail at the constant level; a first version with a fixed Gauss–Hermite rule missed the tail’s narrow transition and was corrected before any outcome at checkpoint R07). Within one law vv estimates 00 and the level is exactly Beta(j,Ncal+1−j)\mathrm{Beta}(j, N_{\mathrm{cal}} + 1 - j) when σC=σE\sigma_C = \sigma_E. Fixed thresholds go through A.2’s predictor on EE; the scheme-level thresholds use their model-implied values zemp=max{zα,σCzα+ρ‾Czβ}z_{\mathrm{emp}} = \max\{z_\alpha,\ \sigma_C z_\alpha + \sqrt{\bar\rho_C}\, z_\beta\} (the empirical threshold is capped at α\alpha on the pp scale, so its zz is bounded below by zαz_\alpha) and zpool=zαρ‾C+σC2z_{\mathrm{pool}} = z_\alpha\sqrt{\bar\rho_C + \sigma_C^2}. T1 compares SDobs/SDpred\mathrm{SD}_{\mathrm{obs}}/\mathrm{SD}_{\mathrm{pred}} with the band [2/3,3/2][2/3, 3/2] by its key-bootstrap interval; T2 compares the sign of Robs=SD(conformal)/SD(nominal)R_{\mathrm{obs}} = \mathrm{SD}(\mathrm{conformal})/\mathrm{SD}(\mathrm{nominal}), on the same keys and texts, with the pre-stated side of RpredR_{\mathrm{pred}} for the material pairs (RpredR_{\mathrm{pred}} outside [2/3,3/2][2/3, 3/2]).

A.6 Transfer controls the difference of means only. Two null laws with identical occupancy weights, vocabulary and MM can give a calibrated level of 00 on the calibration law and 1/21/2 on the deployment law: with V=4V = 4, m=2m = 2, h=0h = 0 and M=2M = 2, let PP draw the text (1,2)(1, 2) or (3,4)(3, 4) and P′P' one of (1,3)(1, 3), (1,4)(1, 4), (2,3)(2, 3), (2,4)(2, 4), each with equal probability, so that every token has occupancy weight 1/41/4 under both; a threshold that rejects a text exactly when its green count exceeds the largest green count among P′P'’s texts (that count plus any amount in (0,1)(0, 1)) rejects nothing on P′P' for every key, while for the keys whose green set is {1,2}\{1, 2\} or {3,4}\{3, 4\}, for which P′P'’s largest count is 11, it rejects the PP text with both tokens green, half of PP’s texts; for the other four green sets the largest count on P′P' is 22 and nothing is rejected on either law. The mean-shift term vv is therefore one component of a transferred level; the other is the score-tail discrepancy between the laws, which the studies measure and never bound.

A.7 The power side. For a prompt cell, an arm, a key kk and a rule, πk(m)=n−1∑i1{pk(xki)<tkm}\pi_k(m) = n^{-1}\sum_i \mathbf 1\{p_k(x_{ki}) < t_k^m\} over the nn = 50 generated texts of the key and Δk(m)=πk(nominal)−πk(m)\Delta_k(m) = \pi_k(\mathrm{nominal}) - \pi_k(m), paired on the same texts (its sign is not empirical; its size and its distribution across keys are). W1 predicts the key-median cost by the threshold shift alone: Δ̂k=G(α)−G(tk)\hat\Delta_k = G(\alpha) - G(t_k) with GG the share of the cell’s pilot pp-values (the pilot law on validation keys, fixed per cell before the lock) strictly below the argument, and the realised key-median with its key-bootstrap interval is consistent when the interval lies inside Δ̂med±c\hat\Delta_{\mathrm{med}} \pm c with c=max(5pp,2×c = \max(5\ \mathrm{pp},\ 2 \times the cluster-bootstrap SE of the predicted median)) recorded before the lock. W2 reads the across-key dispersion of power as the two-way between-key component of the K4×nK_4 \times n table of rejection indicators, v̂=sK2−MSE/n\hat v = s_K^2 - \mathrm{MS}_E/n (Study 1’s locked estimator on the transposed table), SDb=max(v̂,0)\mathrm{SD}_b = \sqrt{\max(\hat v, 0)}, and Rpow=SDb(π(conformal))/SDb(π(nominal))R_{\mathrm{pow}} = \mathrm{SD}_b(\pi(\mathrm{conformal}))/\mathrm{SD}_b(\pi(\mathrm{nominal})) with a paired key-bootstrap interval (200 resamples of the keys, the prompts fixed); the target is the ratio of the prompt-conditioned functionals (the expectation of the raw estimator given the realised prompts), whose population counterpart over prompt samples is an extrapolation; a row is unresolved when the observed denominator is 00 or fewer than 90% of the resamples give a finite ratio. W3 feeds A.5’s predictor the generated null set’s own moments and the calibration prefix’s and compares the across-key SD of the model-null level ℓ̃k\tilde\ell_k over the 1,000 evaluation keys on N0N_0 = 5000 unwatermarked texts per cell with its prediction, by the same band and eligibility.

A.8 The secondary population diagnostic. Studies 1 and 2 also report, per cell, the two-way estimate sK2−MSE/Neffs_K^2 - \mathrm{MS}_E/N_{\mathrm{eff}} of the population across-key variance of the level (so that 𝔼[sK2]=vK+vE/N\mathbb{E}[s_K^2] = v_K + v_E/N) against the predictor’s Var(αk)\operatorname{Var}(\alpha_k), with a crossed key-by-text bootstrap SE (texts and keys resampled; BB = 100). The crossed resample centres at (K−1)/K⋅sK2(K-1)/K \cdot s_K^2 rather than at the corrected component (the R09 F18 identity, checked on the 3,180 of 3,582 stored rows of Study 1 and the 9,724 of 10,404 of Study 2 that carry a positive bootstrap SE and a non-zero component, where the standardised discrepancy of the stored bootstrap mean from the identity has SD close to 1; Table D5); a key-only resample (BB = 2,000) measures instead the uncertainty of the text-conditioned functional (R09 F22), and under a multiplicative key-by-text interaction its SE is far below the population sampling SD. Table D5 gives the counts under both constructions and under normal theory; neither carries a validated population confidence statement.

The paragraphs state the tolerances the code applies where the tables abbreviate them, the two text/code discrepancies, the abstentions, the key samples and the one-correlated-draw statements.

B.1 The adequacy rules (R1, S1, T1, W3). The statistic is the ratio of the observed across-key SD to the predicted one with its key-bootstrap 95% interval (200 resamples of the keys); a cell is consistent when the interval lies inside [2/3, 3/2], inconsistent when it is disjoint from the band and unresolved otherwise; the rule passes with at least 30 resolved cells and at least 80% of the resolved consistent (W3: 30 and 80%). Eligibility: a theory share of at least 0.75, with a mean MM of at least 30 in Studies 1 and 2 and, in Studies 3 and 4, no flag on the prediction and (W3) a positive predicted SD. The candidate, eligible, resolved, consistent, inconsistent and unresolved counts are always reported together (Tables B1–B4); every non-consistent cell or row is listed with its diagnostics in Tables D4a–D4d.

B.2 The audits (R2, S2) and the identities (R3, S3). R2 and S2 compare the key-mean level a‾\bar a with its exact value μa\mu_a given the texts (the mean over texts of the binomial tail under the distinct rule; exactly α\alpha for Gumbel-max) by the two-sided Hoeffding–KL bound min{1,2exp(−KKL(a‾∥μa))}\min\{1, 2\exp(-K\,\mathrm{KL}(\bar a \,\|\, \mu_a))\}, flagged below 10−610^{-6}; the per-check false-audit probability is at most 10−610^{-6} for any KK and, the keys being independent draws of the ideal key law given the texts, under any dependence between texts and between checks or cells (not under dependence between the key draws themselves), and the experiment-level one is at most the number of checks times 10−610^{-6} by the union bound (891 and 3,060 checks, none flagged); the bound cannot flag under-rejection at α=0.01\alpha = 0.01 in most cells and flags a dead detector only where 2(1−μa)K<10−62(1 - \mu_a)^K < 10^{-6}, with the flaggable thresholds tabulated in each study’s validation record. R3 and S3 are the identity checks of Appendix A.1 with the gross-failure threshold 6SE+τ6\,\mathrm{SE} + \tau, τ=10−9g(1−g)A\tau = 10^{-9} g(1-g) A for the green fraction and 10−9A10^{-9} A for the Gumbel-max mean score; structural zeros (the fixed-size variance vanishing when VA−B≤10−9VAV A - B \le 10^{-9} V A) require s02≤τs_0^2 \le \tau; the nominal-95% coverage of the key-bootstrap intervals is a diagnostic. The 6-SE threshold is heuristic: s02s_0^2 is a mean of KK i.i.d. terms (bounded for the green fraction; with finite fourth moments, not bounded, for the Exp(1) scores), central at the studies’ KK by the validation’s disjoint subsamples, and no experiment-level probability is claimed for it.

B.3 Length and width (R4, R4b, R5, S4, S5). The tolerances are Appendix A.3’s. On real text S4 fails in 24 of 100 chains, listed in Table B2: 18 at h=4h = 4 (16 of them outside the news register, where the predictor’s decomposition puts the finite-text term first), 6 news chains at h=1h = 1–44, where the between-text collision itself falls with length, and 2 further h=1h = 1 chains; the three groups overlap, a news chain at h=4h = 4 belonging to both the h=4h = 4 group and the news group. The lattice of the zz-test’s exact size at γ=0.25\gamma = 0.25 under the raw rule, which a reported “false-positive rate at 1%” inherits, is 1.47%, 0.96% and 1.28% at h=1h = 1 for L∈{64,128,256}L \in \{64, 128, 256\} (M=L−1M = L - 1), and 1.54%, 1.02% and 1.35% at h=4h = 4 (MM = 60, 124, 252); a plausible account of the h=4h = 4 dip, not an identified decomposition.

B.4 Study 3’s rules (T1, T2, REP). T2 takes the material pairs (RpredR_{\mathrm{pred}} outside [2/3, 3/2]) and counts a pair as agreeing when the paired key-bootstrap interval of RobsR_{\mathrm{obs}} excludes 1 on the predicted side, disagreeing when it excludes 1 on the other side and unresolved otherwise; it passes with at least 30 resolved and at least 90% agreeing. REP compares Study 3’s nominal across-key SD with Study 2’s on the same texts under independent keys for every (tokenizer, evaluation cell except Gutenberg, width, rule) of the KGW family at α=0.01\alpha = 0.01, a gross failure at |SD3−SD2|>6se32+se22+10−12|\mathrm{SD}_3 - \mathrm{SD}_2| > 6\sqrt{\mathrm{se}_3^2 + \mathrm{se}_2^2} + 10^{-12} (108 checks, 0 gross failures). The diagonal at NcalN_{\mathrm{cal}} = 5,000 is never T1-eligible (the Beta term’s SD is of the order of the finite-evaluation floor, so the theory share lies below the floor) and is reported descriptively against the reference law.

B.5 Study 4’s rules (W1, W2, W3), the record contract and the budget stop. W1’s tolerance is c=max(t,2SE)c = \max(t,\, 2\,\mathrm{SE}) with tt = 5 pp and SE the cluster-bootstrap SE of the predicted key-median, recorded before the lock; it passes with at least 2 of 3 cells consistent and at most 0 inconsistent. W2 passes with at least 5 of 6 rows agreeing and at most 0 disagreeing, the raw rule’s pre-stated side “below 1” and the distinct rule’s “inside [2/3, 3/2]”. W3 is B.1’s rule on the model-null block. Every generation unit’s stored green flags must equal the locked detector’s recomputation at every scored position; a unit that fails is a contract failure and enters no rule (0 of 900 units failed; a run with more than 5% failures would have been void). A committed threshold at or below 0 never rejects (an abstention, never repaired): 6 stored thresholds of Study 3 are zero at a positive order index (a stored pp-value of exactly 0, its numerical cause not isolated), beside the structural abstentions of the tolerance and conformal methods at α=0.001\alpha = 0.001 and small NcalN_{\mathrm{cal}}; Table D10d lists the 481 null-block settings with abstaining keys. A text with rep-4 above 0.5 is a loop, reported and never excluded. The runner’s budget stop (2 times the validator’s projection per invocation) was not reached.

B.6 Disclosed text/code discrepancies. (i) Study 1’s R4b: the locked analyser compared the residual with 3SE+10−93\,\mathrm{SE} + 10^{-9} where the protocol’s text wrote 3SE3\,\mathrm{SE}; the residuals were floating-point remainders of an exactly saturated quantity and the SEs smaller still, so the literal text fails in every cell and the implemented rule passes; the verdict stays the locked rule’s (“reproduced”) and the discrepancy travels with it. (ii) Study 2’s unit-σ\sigma secondary block is listed in the protocol and computed by the report builder, not by the locked analyser; it is descriptive. Every later protocol states every tolerance its code applies.

B.7 Key samples and the one-correlated-draw statements. Study 1 draws its production keys from seed 20,000: 500 keys on the core laws under the independent colouring and 200 under the fixed-size colouring on the core subset (a larger sample on the small-vocabulary illustration laws, which enter no rule), the same seeds and the same hashed token ids across laws, so comparisons across cells are paired and the key-sampling error is common to them; under the fixed colouring the hypergeometric draws are paired across lengths and rules within a chain only. Study 2 draws 1,000 keys from seed 22,000, shared across every cell, width, length, rule, family and tokenizer. Study 3 draws 1,000 evaluation keys from seed 23,000, independent of Study 2’s, and 1,000 disjoint fitting keys from seed 24,000; every method, source, rule, family and width of a cell and tokenizer shares them. Study 4’s power rows use a seeded uniform subset of 100 of Study 3’s evaluation keys (seed 20,261,016) and its null rows all 1,000. Validation keys start at seed 1,000,000, and the reserved confirmatory range 30,000–30,999 is refused by code and was never used. In every study, “n of m cells” counts over such cells are not independent replications.

Appendix C Data

C.1 Sources, terms and licences. The null corpora are four open-content sources read from the Hugging Face Hub after their terms of use and licences were checked and quoted (research/corpora/TERMS_OF_USE_CHECK.md): the Common Pile v0.1 subsets common-pile/news_filtered, common-pile/stackexchange_filtered and common-pile/project_gutenberg_filtered (Kandpal et al. 2025), whose records carry a per-document licence string, and the Wikimedia dumps of 2023-11-01 (wikimedia/wikipedia, the configurations 20231101.<lang> for 6 languages) under the project licence (Wikimedia Foundation 2023). A unit is admitted only with a licence identity on a closed allow-list (cc-by-4.0, cc-by-sa-2.5, cc-by-sa-3.0, cc-by-sa-4.0, public-domain; a Stack Exchange thread needs a non-empty component list with every entry admitted); 19,060 units were excluded by it. The freeze pinned 17 files (6.79 GiB) at their Hub revisions, the local SHA-256 of every data file equal to the Hub’s own LFS digest (outputs/corpora_manifest_v0.1.json); the raw texts stay local and are not redistributed.

C.2 Units, parsing and eligibility. A unit is one news article (17 outlets), one Stack Exchange thread (question, answers and comments; 35 sites), one Project Gutenberg book, whose study window starts at token 1,000, or one Wikipedia article; 2,617,347 units were parsed with no unparseable line, every file reconciled exactly to its card count or Parquet footer (Table C1). Token counts under both tokenizers are taken on a prefix capped at 12,000 characters (24,000 for books) with a censoring flag; a unit is eligible at length LL when its Llama-3.2 count is at least LL (books: 1,000 + LL), and the Qwen2.5-eligible subset is reported beside it.

C.3 Deduplication. Texts are normalised (NFC, lower case, whitespace collapsed, punctuation stripped); exact duplicates are found by the SHA-256 of the normalised text (230 unions) and near duplicates by MinHash (128 permutations) of the 5-gram shingles of the normalised-text prefix with 32 × 4 LSH bands, every distinct band-sharing pair (3,095,016 pairs over 75,295 units, with no estimate-based prefilter) confirmed by the exact Jaccard similarity of the full-text shingle fingerprints at or above 0.65 (85,442 pairs; 7,097 unions); CJK-dominant texts are shingled by characters; clusters are the connected components, computed across every source and language at once (0 cross-source clusters); the analysis unit is one representative per cluster (2,610,020 clusters; 2,590,960 allowed representatives), and the 7,327 non-representative duplicates are never scored. A planted-copy validation under the production predicate and the comparison with the pre-set rule are recorded in the audit (research/corpora/CORPORA_AUDIT_v0.3.md).

C.4 The holdout and the seal. A cluster is held out when the first sixteen hexadecimal digits of the SHA-256 of its identifier, read as an integer, are 0 modulo 5, unless it contains a unit whose text was seen before the partition (73 exposure records; their clusters forced open): 518,060 representatives are held out against 2,072,900 open, 20.00% of the clusters. The sealed accessor binds to the finalised map by its digest and the units-metadata digest, resolves every requested id against the map, opens the held-out partition only for a lock registered in an allow-list that carries final: true, the study name, the manifest, partition and units digests and the cells, and logs every opening; the allow-list is empty, every study’s lock carries final: false, and no opening is logged. The ten confirmatory cells with their eligible counts are in Table C2; the proceed rule asked for at least 2,500 eligible units per cell in each partition and held in every cell.

Every file reconciled exactly to its card count or footer; the held-out share of clusters is 20.00%.

C.5 The texts the studies read. Study 2 draws NN = 10,000 open units per cell by a seeded permutation (seed 20,261,012) through the seal, tokenises them under Llama-3.2-1B (meta-llama/Llama-3.2-1B, revision 4e20de362430cd3b72f300e6b0f18e50e7166e08, VV = 128,256) and Qwen2.5-1.5B (Qwen/Qwen2.5-1.5B, revision 8faed761d45a263340a0528343f099c05c9a4323, VV = 151,936), the same texts under both, and stores the token arrays under a hashed manifest. Study 3 reuses them as the evaluation sets (Gutenberg’s as a 7,122-text prefix) and draws 5,000 further calibration texts per cell as uniform permutation scans conditional on the stated exclusions (the English Wikipedia scans exclude the other English Wikipedia cell’s evaluation ids), with nested prefixes at Ncal∈{500,1,000,2,000,5,000}N_{\mathrm{cal}} \in \{500, 1{,}000, 2{,}000, 5{,}000\}. Study 4 takes fresh units of the three English cells, disjoint from every evaluation and calibration set, as prompts: the first 64 tokens of each, 5,000 per cell, with the human continuation of 192 tokens kept as the quality reference; the model generates 256 new tokens at temperature 1 and top-pp 1 under each arm’s hook for the 50 watermarked prompts per key, and the 5,000 unwatermarked continuations per cell form the model-null set.

C.6 Quality of the generations. No text was excluded. The loop share (rep-4 above 0.5) at the primary arm is 0.02 / 0.08 / 0.46% (news / Wikipedia / Stack Exchange), 0.22 / 0.32 / 1.56% at δ=2.0\delta = 2.0 and 0.02 / 0.10 / 0.58% unwatermarked; the model’s perplexity of its own tokens is 20.9 / 11.2 / 10.8 at the primary arm against 21.1 / 9.7 / 10.6 unwatermarked and 11.6 / 9.5 / 8.9 for the human continuations (Table D9a); the per-prompt pairing is Table D9b.

Appendix D Supplementary tables and figures

Each item is generated from the locked outputs: Tables D1, D14 and D15 by the paper’s table script from the results files (D14 and D15 are the deployer’s view in numbers and the transfer-by-class table that the main text condenses in §5.3 and §6.4); Tables D2a, D3, D4, D6–D13 extracted verbatim from the agreed reports’ generated tables, each caption naming the report, its table number and the checkpoint at which it was agreed; Tables D2b and D5 computed from the two labelled side computations with their values asserted against the registers; Figures D1–D5 are the agreed reports’ own figures, copied unchanged with their provenance recorded, and Figures D6 and D7 are generated by the main figures’ scripts. Items D12 and D13 carry the supplements that Tables D14 and D15 (the former main-text tables) and Figure 5 promise; every ordered transfer pair and every (α, unit) remedy row are in the Study 3 report’s supplements in the companion repository.

D1. The first-order predictor by block (§5.2).

Table D1. Table D2b. The labelled post-hoc overlap account of the across-tokenizer co-movement (the side computation research/study2/tokenizer_overlap.py of 2026-10-13, not pre-registered; its first version compared string-identical ids and was corrected at checkpoint R06, the superseded output kept beside it): per corpus cell at its longest length (L = 256, or 1,024 for the two 1,024-token cells), at h = 0 and h = 1 under the raw rule, over the N = 10,000 realised texts and the stored q̂ values of the K = 1,000 production keys: the number of integer tuple ids shared by the two tokenizers’ document-averaged weight vectors, the share of each tokenizer’s occurrence weight that sits on the shared tuples (Llama-3.2 / Qwen2.5), the identity-predicted correlation of the per-key mean green fraction q̂ from the shared hash inputs, and the observed correlation of the stored q̂ values of the two production cells; the last column is the semantic diagnostic (the share of each tokenizer’s token occurrences on the 410 ids of 128,256 whose token string is the same in both vocabularies), not the hash-overlap estimand. The colouring hash is a function of the integer ids alone, so the identity, not shared strings, accounts for the co-movement; the shared-id account of the cross-language correlations under Qwen2.5 remains a hypothesis.

D5. The secondary population diagnostic with both SE counts (§8; Appendix A.8).

Table D5. Appendix E Locks, records, digests and the review rounds

E.1 The locks. Each study’s lock (PRE_RUN_LOCK.json) was written on the author’s word after the protocol’s checkpoint had ended in the reviewer’s agreement and before any outcome existed: it names the study, the protocol, the grid, the validation record and the agreed resolution memo with their SHA-256 digests, every pinned code file (75 pinned entries over the four locks; Table E2), the key record, approved_by, before_outcomes: true and final: false; from Study 2 on it also pins the earlier studies’ core modules, manifests and run manifests it depends on. The runner verifies the lock against its canonical identity before any study unit and refuses otherwise; every unit carries the lock’s, the grid’s, the code’s and the manifests’ digests and its own slot; the analyser requires the verified lock, derives the provenance every unit must carry from the lock and refuses an incomplete or padded inventory, a foreign unit, a mismatching digest or a unit that fails its record contract (Appendix B.5). A failed rule stays failed; a correction would be a new version with the old one kept.

Table E2. The four pre-run locks and their records: the protocol, the lock date, the checkpoint whose agreement preceded it, the number of pinned files (the protocol, grid, code, validation record, manifests and, from Study 2 on, the earlier studies’ pinned files), the lock’s digest, the commit at launch, the run’s start and duration, the record’s file count and inventory digest, the locked analyser’s results digest, and whether the lock opened the holdout (final). Every lock carries before_outcomes: true and approved_by: Yichen; the runners refuse to start without the verified lock and the analysers recompute under it.

E.2 The records and the builders. The run manifests record the launch commit, the lock’s digest, the run’s start, end and duration, the inventory digest and, for Studies 3 and 4, the locked analyser’s results digest (Study 3’s results file exceeds the repository host’s file limit and is committed gzipped with both digests recorded, the raw file regenerable from the record by the locked analyser). Each report builder reads only the locked record and the analyser’s output, refuses a results file under another lock or with a fixture marker, re-derives every descriptive row, every pass-1 summary and every registered interval under the protocol’s seed convention and asserts equality with the analyser’s file, and writes byte-identical outputs across builds (a fixed SVG hash salt, no timestamps); the paper’s own scripts assert 359 table and 137 figure values against those outputs with 0 and 0 failures. Every result and count in the prose is a placeholder resolved at assembly from those registers, the grids, the locks and the manifests (563 placeholders in 16 section files); a syntactic literal scan then classifies the numerals and counting words its patterns recognise in the prose and inside the formulas, 455 in all (241 of them in formulas): 130 settings written beside their symbol and checked against the pinned grids, 176 constants allow-listed with a reason bound to their phrase (worked examples, other papers’ own numbers, protocol tolerances and pinned code constants verified against their source text), and 149 occurrences of the integers 0, 1 and 2 inside formulas; it sets aside, and counts by class, 1,191 spans that carry a digit or a counting word but are not results (cross-references, 196; identifiers such as rule, checkpoint and claim ids, dates and model names, 275; citation blocks, 124; the deployer’s quantile labels in their labelled form, 16; exponents and subscripts, 84; placeholders, code spans and markers, 479; notation, 9; the adequacy band [2/3, 3/2], asserted against the pinned grids, 8); the assembly refuses the draft on any recognised literal outside these classes, and the scan carries a self-test of the sentences that escaped its earlier versions. The scan is a syntactic check with these documented exclusions: a numeral written in a form outside its patterns is not seen by it, and the drafts were read for such cases by the author at the section reviews and by the independent reviewer at the rounds of checkpoint R12. The data access layer is described in Appendix C.4.

E.3 The independent review. Under rule A7 of the project’s guide, at every major checkpoint the conductor (the drafting model) wrote a prompt naming the scope, the files to read in order and the memo to write; the reviewer (Codex) wrote a memo of claims against evidence, method and statistics, process, an independent search and numbered findings with a verdict; the conductor verified every alleged error against the primary evidence before acting, fixed the facts and logged them as its own corrections, put every judgement call to the author with the reviewer’s recommendation first, and answered in a response memo; the rounds continued until AGREE with no open dispute, recorded in a resolution memo that names the agreed commit. Table E1 lists the 13 checkpoints (R01–R13: the topic lock, the data verdict, each study’s protocol before its lock, each study’s report, the claim gate and this draft) with their 40 rounds (2 to 5 per checkpoint) and 212 findings; 0 checkpoints were agreed at their first round.

Table E1. The independent second-model review (rule A7 of the project’s guide): every major checkpoint was reviewed by Codex before the project proceeded, and none proceeded without its AGREE with no open dispute. Rounds and findings are counted from the memos in research/reviews/; a finding is a numbered item of a memo (F1, F2, …), each verified by the conductor against the primary evidence before any change and answered in a response memo; the agreed baseline is the commit the reviewer read at the agreeing round. 13 checkpoints (R01–R13), 40 rounds, 212 findings; 0 in progress.
